EXPERIMENTAL SECTIONMaterials. Carbon nanotubes (CNTs) and sulfur powder were purchased from Macklin Biochem. (China) and Aladdin (China), respectively. Ammonia (25 wt.%) and acetone were supplied by Beijing Chemical Reagents (China). Formaldehyde (37 wt.%) and aminophenol were purchased from the Energy Chemicals (China). All chemicals were used as received and without further purification. Syntheses of sCA Compounds and Modification of Separators. The preparation process of sCA compounds was similar to previous work.[1] Briefly, 45 mg of CNTs were dispersed in deionized water/ethanol solvent, followed by adding 0.7 mL of ammonia catalyst, 0.6 mg of aminophenol, and 0.6 mL of formaldehyde in sequence. Acetone was added to terminate the reaction to get the CA compound. Finally, an inverse sulfurization method was used to synthesize sCA compound by heating the mixture of CA and sulfur powder under an argon atmosphere. The sCA modified PP (sCAP) separator were fabricated by coating the mixture of sCA and PVDF (9:1) on the PP separators.Characterization. Chemical compositions of sCA and sCAP were characterized using a Rigaku D/Max 2500 X-ray diffractometer, a Thermo Fischer DXR Raman microscopy, a Thermo K-alpha X-ray photoelectron spectroscopy. The ION-TOF Gmhb 5 time of flight-secondary ion mass spectroscopy was used to explore the specific chemical structures of sCA and sCAP. Microstructures and morphologies of sCA and sCAP were observed with a FEI Talos F200x transmission electron microscope and a HITACHI S4700 scanning electron microscope. The Vario EL III elemental analysis and a TA Instruments Q50 thermogravimetric analyzer (TGA) were carried to evaluate sulfur contents. Specific surface areas were measured using an AutoPore Iv 9510 analyzer on the basis of the Brunauer-Emmett-Teller (BET) method. The contact angle was measured by a Dataphysics OCA20 tester. The signal of lithium polysulfides was detected with a Shimadzu UV-3600 UV-vis spectrophotometer.Measurements of Electrochemical Performances. Electrochemical performances were tested by 2025-type coin cells. The mixture of sulfur powder, super P, and PVDF as mass ratio 7:2:1 was coated onto an aluminum foil, and then dried at 60 oC under vacuum overnight. The working electrodes were cut into 10 mm diameter with sulfur loading of 1.2 mg cm-2; for high-loading test, the sulfur loading is about 4.6 and 6.8 mg cm-2. The electrolyte is 1 M LiTFSI in DOL/DME (1:1, v:v) with 2 wt% LiNO3. During electrochemical performance test, the electrolyte/sulfur ratio was about 15 uL/mg[S]; for high-loading test, the electrolyte amount was about 30 uL. Cycling and rate performances were measured with a Land CT 2000A battery testing system, and cyclic voltammograms curves and electrochemical impedance spectra were obtained on a CHI 660E electrochemical workstation.Adsorption of Li2S4. The 1 M Li2S4 solution was obtained by dissolving S powder and Li2S with a molar ratio of 3:1 in DOL/DME solution, and then vigorous stirring at room temperature for 24 h. For adsorption test, 10 mg sCA or cCA was dispersed into 10 mL 5 mM Li2S4 solution. After adsorption for 24 h, the optical image and UV-vis spectra were obtained using a digital camera and a Shimadzu UV-3600 UV-vis spectrophotometer, respectively.Li2S6 Symmetric Cells and Nucleation of Li2S. The 1 M Li2S6 solution was obtained by dissolving S powder and Li2S with a molar ratio of 5:1 in electrolyte solution, and then vigorous stirring at room temperature for 24 h. The carbon fiber cloth (CC) with a diameter of 12 mm was prepared. The sCA or cCA compounds were coated onto CC with mass loading of 0.5 mg cm-2. For CV test of Li2S6 symmetric cells, the sCA or cCA compounds were used as working electrodes with a Celgard 2325 as the separator. The amount of electrolyte containing 0.5 M Li2S6 was 40 uL; for blank test, the electrolyte was no Li2S6. CV of Li2S6 symmetrical cells were performed on a CHI 660E electrochemical workstation at -0.8 - 0.8 V with scan rate from 1 to 10 mV s-1. For nucleation of Li2S, the sCA or cCA compounds were used as working electrodes, and Li foil was used as the counter electrode. Th
easurements. The cells with a Be window, provided by the Beijing Science Star technology, were used for in situ XRD analyses. The assembly of in situ batteries was similar to normal button batteries. For the XRD analysis, the battery was cycling at 0.1 C and the XRD signals was recorded with a step size of 1o min-1.Computation method. The first principle calculations are performed by Vienna Ab initio Simulation Package (VASP) with the projector augmented wave (PAW) method. The exchange-functional is treated using the Perdew-Burke-Ernzerhof (PBE) functional, in combination with the DFT-D correction. The calculations were performed in a spin-polarized manner. The cut-off energy of the plane-wave basis is set at 500 eV. For the optimization of both geometry and lattice size, the Brillouin zone integration is performed with 3*3*3 Monkhorts-Pack kpoint sampling. The self-consistent calculations apply a convergence energy threshold of 10-5 eV. The equilibrium geometries and lattice constants are optimized with maximum stress on each atom within 0.02 eV/

Nyquist plots of EIS start with an Ohmic resistance (R1) determined by the ionic conductivity of electrolyte and the electronic conductivity between active material particles. Two depressed semicircles in high frequency and middle high frequency regions correspond to interface resistance (R2) and charge transfer resistance (R3), respectively.
